    AnyGameRenderer: Entity-Consistent Generative Rendering Across Games and an Entity-Level Benchmark

    Authors: Ruicong Liu, Liangyang Ouyang, Fengbo Lan, Zhixiang Wang, Kaipeng Zhang

    Abstract: …G-buffers, per-object class labels, or low-poly proxies. Gray blockouts, colored primitives, semantic maps, and wireframes all work as input. It also preserves entity correspondence: group counts stay correct, and entities appear and disappear on the right frame. We implement AnyGameRenderer by LoRA fine-tuning MiniMax-H3 ref2va, a 33B reference-video-condit… ▽ More AI coding agents can now build small playable games in minutes, but the results usually look like programmer art: gray blockouts, colored geometric primitives, and flat semantic blobs. We present AnyGameRenderer, a video-to-video generative renderer that turns such footage into high-quality visuals with characters, motion detail, and effects. Throughout, it keeps the layout, camera, and every entity's position and timing intact. Our AnyGameRenderer generalizes across a wide range of genres that agents build, such as tower defense, auto-battlers, top-down survivors, turn-based battles, kart racing, and crowd runners. AnyGameRenderer takes plain video as its game-state input, with no complex representations such as engine G-buffers, per-object class labels, or low-poly proxies. Gray blockouts, colored primitives, semantic maps, and wireframes all work as input. It also preserves entity correspondence: group counts stay correct, and entities appear and disappear on the right frame. We implement AnyGameRenderer by LoRA fine-tuning MiniMax-H3 ref2va, a 33B reference-video-conditioned video generation model. Training uses ~10k paired clips from three sources: (i) multi-stream rendering data from 38 agent-made games; (ii) two-stream rendering data from Unreal Engine, with programmatically controlled crowds, count changes, and entity scales; and (iii) real gameplay recordings, with pairs reconstructed via 3D-box detection. To evaluate the entity correspondence, we propose an entity-level benchmark of 59 held-out cases with 753 entities, scored against ground-truth boxes. Compared with the base model, AnyGameRenderer better preserves entity correspondence: temporal match rises from 50.9% to 62.9%, missed count changes fall from 34.7% to 21.3%, and fewer entities are hallucinated or left unrendered. Project page: https://alaya-lab.github.io/AnyGameRenderer. △ Less

    IntReal: A Real-World Dataset and Benchmark for Multimodal Interleaved Generation

    Authors: Shaohan Yu, Jie Qin, Wende Tan, Haiwen Diao, Limeng Qiao, Yueming Lyu, Chenyang Si

    Abstract: …interleaved generation remains largely underexplored. Progress in this area is hindered by two key limitations: (1) the lack of well-defined interleaved generation tasks and corresponding training data, and (2) the absence of fine-grained evaluation protocols that can reliably diagnose model performance across different capability dimensions. To this end, we… ▽ More Humans routinely consume multimodal content, where textual and visual information is naturally interleaved. Although Unified Multimodal Models (UMMs) can jointly generate text and images, multimodal interleaved generation remains largely underexplored. Progress in this area is hindered by two key limitations: (1) the lack of well-defined interleaved generation tasks and corresponding training data, and (2) the absence of fine-grained evaluation protocols that can reliably diagnose model performance across different capability dimensions. To this end, we present IntReal, comprising two complementary components: IntReal-100K, a real-world dataset of multimodal interleaved samples spanning four major categories, and the IntReal Evaluation Suite, which evaluates model performance across seven complementary dimensions using specialized expert models, enabling coarse-to-fine diagnosis. Extensive experiments validate our evaluation suite through low inter-metric redundancy (max pairwise absolute Spearman's $ρ\leq 0.42$ ), high agreement with human judgments (83.3% accuracy), and strong consistency across different expert evaluators (Spearman's $ρ> 0.9$ ), supporting the complementarity and reliability of our metrics. By benchmarking four families of UMMs, we show that IntReal Evaluation Suite reveals distinct strengths and weaknesses across models. Post-training experiments demonstrate the utility of IntReal-100K for improving interleaved generation and further show that training unimodal generation can also benefit interleaved generation. These findings suggest that interleaved generation can benefit from modality-specific optimization, rather than requiring joint text-image training at every stage. △ Less

    Logarithmic fractional Hardy inequalities

    Authors: Michał Kijaczko, Vivek Sahu

    Abstract: …is a logarithmic-type weight and the constant is sharp. We also show that the corresponding inequality with the simpler weight $|x|^{-d}(\log(R/|x|))^{-p}$ cannot hold with any positive constant. Furthermore, we derive two-sided estimates and asymptotic formulae for $W_{p,d,R}$, including its limiting connection with the classical Hardy inequality. In the h… ▽ More We establish sharp fractional Hardy inequalities in the critical regimes $sp=d$ and $sp=1$, with explicit logarithmic weight functions. In the whole-space setting, for $p>d\ge 1$, $sp=d$, we prove that \begin{equation*} \int_{\mathbb R^d} \int_{\mathbb R^d} \frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^p}{|x-y|^{2d}}\,dx\,dy \geq \left(\frac{p-1}{p}\right)^p \int_{B_R}|u(x)|^p W_{p,d,R}(x)\,dx, \end{equation*} for $u\in C_c(B_R)$, where $W_{p,d,R}$ is a logarithmic-type weight and the constant is sharp. We also show that the corresponding inequality with the simpler weight $|x|^{-d}(\log(R/|x|))^{-p}$ cannot hold with any positive constant. Furthermore, we derive two-sided estimates and asymptotic formulae for $W_{p,d,R}$, including its limiting connection with the classical Hardy inequality. In the half-space $\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}$, we establish an analogous sharp fractional Hardy inequality in the critical case $sp=1$, with an explicit logarithmic weight depending on the distance to the boundary. We also investigate the asymptotic behaviour of this weight, obtaining estimates that describe its behaviour near the boundary. These results elucidate the role of logarithmic weights in critical fractional Hardy inequalities in both whole-space and half-space settings. △ Less

    Using delays for prescribed-time static output feedback stabilization of LTI controllable and observable systems

    Authors: Kang-Kang Zhang, Emilia Fridman

    Abstract: …design is further extended to known bounded and fast-varying (without any constraints on the delay derivative) measurement delays, yielding fixed-time stabilization under a corresponding rank condition, with an assignable settling-time bound greater than three times the delay bound. For unknown constant measurement delays, fixed-time stabilization is establi… ▽ More This paper addresses a stabilization problem: can every controllable and observable LTI system be stabilized by static output feedback? To the best of our knowledge, we provide, for the first time, a solution to this problem. Furthermore, the proposed design achieves prescribed-time convergence to the origin, with the convergence time arbitrarily assigned a priori and independently of the initial condition. Two static output-feedback methods are developed. The first employs multiple artificial output delays with smooth time-varying gains and provides an explicit gain construction for every controllable and observable LTI system, based on a generalized controllability--observability Gramian introduced in this paper. The second combines constant-gain feedback of the current output with time-varying-gain feedback of a single artificially delayed output and also applies to every controllable and observable LTI system. Its simpler structure requires auxiliary parameters whose existence is guaranteed but whose explicit construction is not provided for general MIMO systems; for SISO systems, the constant current-output gain can be readily selected. The multiple-delay design is further extended to known bounded and fast-varying (without any constraints on the delay derivative) measurement delays, yielding fixed-time stabilization under a corresponding rank condition, with an assignable settling-time bound greater than three times the delay bound. For unknown constant measurement delays, fixed-time stabilization is established for systems with nilpotent state matrices. A numerical implementation is also presented. Numerical examples involving nonminimum-phase, flexible-structure, and cart--pole systems illustrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods. △ Less

    Abstract: …comments (danmaku), and 13,739 cover images. Weekly issues provide a consistent unit for tracing changes in category composition, recurring creators and editorial framing. Corresponding comments and danmaku connect video-level discussion with audience expressions tied to particular moments, while covers add a visual dimension. We evaluate retrieval coverage… ▽ More BiliPicks documents seven years of Bilibili's Weekly Picks, a platform-curated editorial video series. The dataset covers 371 publicly listed issues and 13,758 curated videos from 22 March 2019 to 7 May 2026. It links issue dates, themes and editorial recommendations to video metadata, 47,799,657 top-level comments, 125,690,760 time-synchronised overlay comments (danmaku), and 13,739 cover images. Weekly issues provide a consistent unit for tracing changes in category composition, recurring creators and editorial framing. Corresponding comments and danmaku connect video-level discussion with audience expressions tied to particular moments, while covers add a visual dimension. We evaluate retrieval coverage and illustrate longitudinal analyses of selection, audience language and cover presentation. BiliPicks provides a dataset for future studies of platform curation, linking changes in published selections to their editorial framing and corresponding audience engagement. △ Less

    Structure-retained low-rank adapters for weather synthesis

    Authors: Shunxin Wang, Alexandros Stergiou, Luuk Spreeuwers, Estefania Talavera, Nicola Strisciuglio

    Abstract: …relevant structural information is mostly processed during image-to-image translation. Through an architectural analysis of SDXL, we learn structural LoRA matrices for the corresponding blocks and then leverage them for weather synthesis, eliminating the need for external conditioning such as ControlNet. Experimental results demonstrate that our method impro… ▽ More Real-world deployment of computer vision models, particularly in outdoor scenarios, is often limited by environmental interferences, such as adverse weather. Improving model robustness across weather conditions is thus critical for reliability. However, sourcing high-quality data relevant to isolated weather effects is a significant challenge. Most datasets include changes in dynamic elements across observations of the same scene: subjects such as pedestrians and vehicles change between shots, while camera viewpoints vary. These aspects make it difficult to decouple solely weather-induced distribution shifts from the scene. In this work, we address this with a novel weather synthesis framework that utilizes structure-retaining Low-Rank Adapters (LoRA). We analyze the attention blocks in Stable Diffusion XL (SDXL) and identify where relevant structural information is mostly processed during image-to-image translation. Through an architectural analysis of SDXL, we learn structural LoRA matrices for the corresponding blocks and then leverage them for weather synthesis, eliminating the need for external conditioning such as ControlNet. Experimental results demonstrate that our method improves upon existing synthesis approaches, achieving greater realism while preserving structure. This provides a robust pipeline for generating high-fidelity training and evaluation data for outdoor computer vision applications. △ Less

    Amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay Lambda_c+ to p K_S^0 pi0

    Authors: M. Ablikim, M. N. Achasov, P. Adlarson, X. C. Ai, C. S. Akondi, R. Aliberti, A. Amoroso, L. P. An, Q. An, M. S. Anderson, Y. Bai, O. Bakina, H. R. Bao, X. L. Bao, M. Barbagiovanni, V. Batozskaya, K. Begzsuren, N. Berger, M. Berlowski, M. B. Bertani, D. Bettoni, F. Bianchi, E. Bianco, A. Bortone, I. Boyko , et al. (763 additional authors not shown)

    Abstract: …annihilation data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 4.5 fb$^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 4.600 and 4.699 GeV, the first amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay $Λ_c^+ \to p K_S^0 π^0$ is performed. The processes $Λ_c^+ \to p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0$, $Λ_c^+ \to Δ(1600)^+ K_S^0$, and… ▽ More Using $e^+e^-$ annihilation data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 4.5 fb$^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 4.600 and 4.699 GeV, the first amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay $Λ_c^+ \to p K_S^0 π^0$ is performed. The processes $Λ_c^+ \to p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0$, $Λ_c^+ \to Δ(1600)^+ K_S^0$, and $Λ_c^+ \to N(1710)^+ K_S^0$ are identified with statistical significances of $12.8σ$, $14.5σ$, and $6.2σ$, respectively. The mass and width of $Δ(1600)^+$ are measured to be $(1520.4 \pm 8.8 \pm 37.7)~\text{MeV}$ and $(199.4 \pm 26.4 \pm 39.9)~\text{MeV}$, respectively. The mass and width of $N(1710)^+$ are measured to be $(1719.7 \pm 9.4 \pm 9.1)~\text{MeV}$ and $(72.0 \pm 18.0 \pm 15.0)~\text{MeV}$, respectively. The branching fractions of these processes are determined to be $\mathcal{B}[Λ_c^+ \rightarrow Δ(1600)^+ (\rightarrow p π^0) K_S^0] = (8.10 \pm 0.80 \pm 5.57) \times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{B}[Λ_c^+ \rightarrow N(1710)^+ (\rightarrow p π^0) K_S^0] = (1.58 \pm 0.48 \pm 0.77) \times 10^{-3}$, and $\mathcal{B}[Λ_c^+ \rightarrow p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0 (\rightarrow K_S^0 π^0)] = (2.63 \pm 0.47 \pm 0.29) \times 10^{-3}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic. △ Less

    Beyond Fresnel Approximation: Exact Airy Beamforming for Near-Field THz Communications

    Authors: Fangjie Shen, Weidong Mei, Aoran Liu, Peilan Wang, Zhi Chen

    Abstract: …propagation distances. To address this limitation, this letter proposes a more accurate Airy beamforming design method based on the exact focal-distance expression and the corresponding caustic geometry. Specifically, we jointly optimize the bending curvature, virtual focal distance, and steering angle to maximize the received signal power subject to receive… ▽ More Line-of-sight (LoS) blockage can severely degrade the performance of conventional focused beams in terahertz (THz) communications. Recently, bending beams (e.g., Airy beams) have attracted increasing attention due to their ability to bypass obstacles by propagating along curved trajectories under near-field propagation conditions. However, existing Airy-beam designs in wireless communications often rely on the Fresnel approximation for the focal distance, which becomes inaccurate at large steering angles or short propagation distances. To address this limitation, this letter proposes a more accurate Airy beamforming design method based on the exact focal-distance expression and the corresponding caustic geometry. Specifically, we jointly optimize the bending curvature, virtual focal distance, and steering angle to maximize the received signal power subject to receiver-alignment and obstacle-avoidance constraints. To tackle the resulting non-convex problem, we first use geometric preprocessing to narrow the feasible range of the bending curvature. Then, a two-layer optimization framework is developed with the outer and inner layers optimizing the bending curvature and the other two parameters, respectively. Numerical results based on the cascaded geometrical and wave channel model demonstrate that the proposed method achieves higher received signal power than conventional Airy-beam designs and offers complementary advantages over conventional focused beams under different blockage ratios. △ Less

    Tracing Electromagnetic Response through Charge Splitting of Directed Flow in the Quark-Gluon Plasma

    Authors: Ankit Kumar Panda, Zhu Meng, Long-Gang Pang, Xin-Nian Wang

    Abstract: …further predicts the mass-dependent charge splittings for identified pions, kaons, and protons, providing further constraints on their respective relaxation times and the corresponding electromagnetic interaction. These results establish the charge splitting of directed flow as a quantitative probe of the electromagnetic response and the space-time evolution… ▽ More Experimentally observed charge splitting of directed flow, $Δv_1\equiv v_1(h^+)-v_1(h^-)$, exhibits both a sign reversal at midrapidity and the amplitude change from Au+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=200$ GeV to Pb+Pb at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=5.02$ TeV. We study this behavior within a unified $(3+1)$-dimensional viscous hydrodynamic model that incorporates an electromagnetic source term in the bulk evolution of the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) and an electromagnetic correction to the Cooper--Frye particlization. The beam-energy dependence of $Δv_1$ can be described by varying the effective electrical conductivity between RHIC and LHC energies, with values consistent with the conductivity scale indicated by lattice-QCD calculations. We find that the sign reversal emerges from the competition between momentum distortions induced by the electric and magnetic field which is controlled by the conductivity during the QGP evolution. The model further predicts the mass-dependent charge splittings for identified pions, kaons, and protons, providing further constraints on their respective relaxation times and the corresponding electromagnetic interaction. These results establish the charge splitting of directed flow as a quantitative probe of the electromagnetic response and the space-time evolution of the QGP. △ Less

    Semantic Anchor Communication System for Point Cloud Transmission in Internet of Vehicles

    Authors: Ruixing Ren, Junhui Zhao, Wei Zhao, Gaoxin Zhang, Xiaoming Wang

    Abstract: …anchors with explicit coordinates are employed as transmission units. Local shape descriptors and category labels are bound to a unified spatial reference, preserving the correspondence between local position, shape and semantics. At the transmitter, anchor representations are extracted and then converted into digital frames through fixed-point quantization… ▽ More Joint transmission of point cloud geometry and semantics under bandwidth constraints is required for cooperative perception in the Internet of Vehicles (IoV). Per-point semantics cannot be explicitly preserved by existing geometric compression methods, while interpretable three-dimensional point sets cannot be directly reconstructed via task-feature communication. The paper proposes a spatial semantic anchor communication system. Distinct from global codewords and task features, anchors with explicit coordinates are employed as transmission units. Local shape descriptors and category labels are bound to a unified spatial reference, preserving the correspondence between local position, shape and semantics. At the transmitter, anchor representations are extracted and then converted into digital frames through fixed-point quantization and lossless compression.Atthereceiver,labeledpoint clouds are reconstructed through anchor-constrained local expansion, and pose-aligned multi-view observations are fused. On the 500-frame SemanticPOSS test set, the results demonstrate that spatial semantic anchors enable interpretable joint reconstruction and fusion of geometry and semantics with low transmission overhead. △ Less

    Uniqueness and radial nondegeneracy of nodal states for a mixed-power scalar field equation

    Authors: Zhibo Cheng, Sunan Wang

    Abstract: …, the corresponding normalized solutions form a global $C^1$ branch. ▽ More We consider the scalar field equation $$\begin{cases} -Δu+u+λ|u|^{q-1}u =μ|u|^{p-1}u, & x\in\mathbb R^n,\\[2mm] u(x)\to 0, & |x|\to\infty, \end{cases}$$ where $n\geq3$, $1<q<p<(n+2)/(n-2)$, and $λ,μ>0$. We study uniqueness and radial nondegeneracy of its sign-changing radial bound states. The proof combines a comparison argument for the shooting variation across nodal intervals with uniform compactness estimates for families of bounded nodal number and continuation in a normalized parameter. We show that, for every integer $k\geq1$, fixing the centre and requiring $u(0)>0$ leaves exactly one radial bound state with $k$ nodes. Its radial linearized operator has trivial kernel in the radial space. For each fixed $k$, the corresponding normalized solutions form a global $C^1$ branch. △ Less

    Abstract: …is the compression quality factor. Compared with the corresponding lower bounds of $Ω(ω^{-1/2}nρ^{-1/4}\sqrt{T})$ and $Ω(ω^{-1}nρ^{-1/2}\ln T)$, these upper bounds exhibit polynomial gaps in their dependence on $ρ^{-1}$. To close these gaps, we first develop Ascent-gossip, which combines an online compression strategy with a Chebyshev recursion and attains… ▽ More In this paper, we investigate decentralized online convex optimization with compressed communication, where $n$ learners over a network jointly minimize a sequence of global loss functions based only on their local losses and compressed messages from their neighbors. The best known regret bounds for this problem are $O(ω^{-1/2}nρ^{-1}\sqrt{T\ln n})$ and $O(ω^{-1}nρ^{-2}\ln n\ln T)$ for convex and strongly convex loss functions, respectively, where $ρ\in(0,1)$ is the spectral gap of the communication matrix and $ω\in(0,1]$ is the compression quality factor. Compared with the corresponding lower bounds of $Ω(ω^{-1/2}nρ^{-1/4}\sqrt{T})$ and $Ω(ω^{-1}nρ^{-1/2}\ln T)$, these upper bounds exhibit polynomial gaps in their dependence on $ρ^{-1}$. To close these gaps, we first develop Ascent-gossip, which combines an online compression strategy with a Chebyshev recursion and attains a faster consensus rate than existing compressed gossip methods. Furthermore, by adopting Ascent-gossip, we propose an online algorithm that achieves optimal regret bounds of $\widetilde O(ω^{-1/2}nρ^{-1/4}\sqrt{T})$ and $\widetilde O(ω^{-1}nρ^{-1/2}\ln T)$ for convex and strongly convex loss functions, respectively. Up to logarithmic factors in $n$ and $ρ^{-1}$, these results match the lower bounds in terms of $T$, $n$, $ρ$, and $ω$. △ Less

    Strict Frequency Bandwidths from Causal Light Cones Enable Scalable Exact Gradient Evaluation in Quantum Optimization

    Authors: Zhaoyang Song, Wei Zhang, Ji Cao, Xuexi Yi, Hong-Fu Wang

    Abstract: …is encoded by the contact region in the interaction line graph. At fixed circuit depth on graphs of bounded degree, the number of distinct circuit settings required for the corresponding derivative collapses from $O(m)$ to $O(1)$. This shows that dynamical locality is not merely a constraint on correlation propagation, but also a computational resource that… ▽ More Dynamical locality is often regarded as a limitation of shallow quantum circuits, yet we show that it can reduce the number of circuit settings required for exact evaluation of gradients with respect to shared parameters. For layered ansatze generated by commuting local terms, strict Heisenberg light cones confine energy slices obtained by varying one shared layer parameter to finite Fourier bandwidths dictated by local causal geometry. This bandlimit recasts exact gradient evaluation as a local signal recovery problem and, when the relevant frequencies lie on a known frequency lattice, yields an exact multipoint parameter-shift rule based on the discrete sine transform. The quantum approximate optimization algorithm (QAOA) provides a canonical realization in which this mechanism is governed by the local structure of the graph. Specifically, when a cost angle is varied, the relevant geometry is encoded by the contact region in the interaction line graph. At fixed circuit depth on graphs of bounded degree, the number of distinct circuit settings required for the corresponding derivative collapses from $O(m)$ to $O(1)$. This shows that dynamical locality is not merely a constraint on correlation propagation, but also a computational resource that enables exact gradient evaluation with fewer circuit settings in quantum optimization. △ Less

    DRC-TTS: A Dual-Resolution Codec-Based Architecture for Lexicon-Free Malay Speech Synthesis

    Authors: Tianhui Su, Tien-Ping Tan

    Abstract: …When predicted character durations are quantized into frame counts at 12.5 Hz, durations shorter than one codec frame may be assigned zero frames. Consequently, the corresponding character representations may be lost during length regulation, potentially leading to pronunciation omissions. This study proposes DRC-TTS, a dual-resolution codec-based architect… ▽ More Modern discrete neural audio codecs facilitate speech synthesis by replacing continuous acoustic representations with low-frame-rate discrete token sequences. For Malay speech synthesis, character-level input reduces reliance on pronunciation lexicons. However, combining character-level inputs with low-frame-rate discrete codecs introduces a temporal alignment challenge. When predicted character durations are quantized into frame counts at 12.5 Hz, durations shorter than one codec frame may be assigned zero frames. Consequently, the corresponding character representations may be lost during length regulation, potentially leading to pronunciation omissions. This study proposes DRC-TTS, a dual-resolution codec-based architecture for lexicon-free Malay speech synthesis. The model expands character representations at a temporal resolution of 50 Hz and subsequently downsamples them through strided convolution to 12.5 Hz for discrete codec-token prediction. Objective evaluations show that the proposed model reduces the character error rate (CER) from 27.11% for the baseline to 21.86%, an absolute reduction of 5.25 percentage points. The proposed model also achieves a mean opinion score (MOS) of 4.30, compared with 4.12 for the baseline. These results suggest that the dual-resolution design may improve character-level alignment and perceived speech naturalness under the evaluated conditions. △ Less

    Game-Theoretic Foundations for Undiscounted Bisimulation Metrics

    Authors: Matthew Zurek, Yudong Chen, Josiah P. Hanna

    Abstract: …operator-centric view with a game-centric one: we interpret prior metric operators as Bellman-Shapley operators of zero-sum turn-based games and define metrics as the corresponding game value functions, yielding unique definitions even when $γ=1$. This game-theoretic foundation facilitates transfer of dynamic-programming theory and algorithms. We also use th… ▽ More Bisimulation metrics offer a principled notion of task-relevant state similarity in reinforcement learning, but existing constructions and methods are largely confined to discounted MDPs, where contraction arguments ensure well-defined fixed points and downstream guarantees. We develop a unified foundation for bisimulation metrics in undiscounted settings, that is total- and average-reward problems, by replacing the standard operator-centric view with a game-centric one: we interpret prior metric operators as Bellman-Shapley operators of zero-sum turn-based games and define metrics as the corresponding game value functions, yielding unique definitions even when $γ=1$. This game-theoretic foundation facilitates transfer of dynamic-programming theory and algorithms. We also use this game-centric perspective to characterize how metric finiteness depends on structural properties of the underlying MDP and motivate certain directed metrics that remain finite in weakly communicating MDPs. We further introduce an abstract framework for modifying the games' action spaces, allowing general transition coupling sets, that recovers many existing metrics and guides new constructions. Finally, we prove that these metrics bound total-reward and, when the optimal gain is state-independent, optimal relative value differences. We also provide error and suboptimality guarantees for aggregation-based planning in total- and average-reward settings. △ Less

    Combining Adaptive Seamless Phase II/III Designs and Group Sequential Analysis for Time-to-Event Endpoints in Oncology Trials

    Authors: Wentian Guo, Qingyu Chen, Chi Song, Jiabu Ye, Chintu Desai, Victor Zhang, Rui Miao

Existing proposals index the boundaries for the post-selection looks by the overall information fraction of the primary endpoint---the correct index for the log-rank statistics of an unadapted trial, but not for the combination statistics these designs actually test---and support family-wise error rate (FWER) control by simulation. We instead work from the joint distribution of the combination statistics themselves, calibrating group sequential boundaries separately for each hypothesis in the closure and applying closed testing at every look; a general lemma establishes that this controls the FWER in the strong sense, for any number of hypotheses and analysis times. Specifically, under patient-wise separation and an inverse-normal combination test, the interim and final statistics for an individual hypothesis are bivariate normal, with a correlation determined by how the events at each look divide between the pre- and post-selection cohorts rather than by the overall information fraction, so that their boundaries follow from standard group sequential software; the corresponding statistics for the intersection hypothesis, formed from a Hochberg-adjusted Stage-1 $p$-value, are not multivariate normal, and we obtain their boundaries by a dedicated numerical integration. Head-to-head against a recently proposed incremental-Dunnett procedure, the two were near-identical when the design projections were correct, but ours was more robust when the combination weights were based on a misprojected division of the Stage~1 events either side of the interim. △ Less

    A Witnessing Duality for ReLU Network Depth

    Authors: Dimitrios Myrisiotis

    Abstract: …for the network but not for the maximum, so one of finitely many test points exposes the error. By witnessing, provable refutation corresponds to a provably correct refuter, and provable existence of a certified network to a provably correct constructor. On the construction side, we introduce certificates that label each maximum gadget by the inputs it aggr… ▽ More A depth lower bound for ReLU networks asserts that every shallower network errs on some input; we ask when a weak theory can prove such a bound so that an error can be computed. For the exact computation of $\max(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$, recent work shows two hidden layers suffice with unrestricted rational weights. By contrast, Haase, Hertrich and Loho (ICLR 2023) proved that integer weights require depth logarithmic in $n$, and Averkov, Hojny and Merkert (ICLR 2025) extended the bound to nonbias weights whose denominators are powers of a fixed integer not divisible by a prime $q$. With integer weights the bound is sharp: the classical balanced construction has depth $\lceil\log_2 n\rceil$. For each fixed $q$ and depth cap $K$, we prove this known bound in $\mathbf{VTC}^0$, the bounded-arithmetic theory of uniform constant-depth threshold computation, together with a refuter that is itself such a computation and whose correctness the theory proves. Given a network with at most $K$ hidden layers, nonbias weights whose reduced denominators avoid $q$, arbitrary rational biases and $n>q^K$ inputs, the refuter returns a rational input of polynomial length on which the network errs. The proof makes the obstruction finite: an iterated intersection product of piecewise-linear functions is divisible by $q$ for the network but not for the maximum, so one of finitely many test points exposes the error. By witnessing, provable refutation corresponds to a provably correct refuter, and provable existence of a certified network to a provably correct constructor. On the construction side, we introduce certificates that label each maximum gadget by the inputs it aggregates; with them the theory verifies the balanced construction, which meets the refuter at the sharp threshold $n\le2^K$, and a rational family of polynomial description length and depth $O(\log n/\log\log n)$ built from known depth-two blocks. △ Less

    Abstract: …interest rate sensitivities. We first derive an exact decomposition of MBS prices into three components: the present value of interest-rate-independent cash flows, the present value of principal repaid early through interest-rate-dependent prepayments, and an interest rate option component capturing the reduction in future cash flows due to prepayments. Under a first-order approximation, the latter two components resemble portfolios of floorlets and Asian-style interest rate options, respectively. We then apply a cumulant expansion to derive closed-form valuation approximations under the one-factor Hull--White model. Numerical results show that including the second cumulant improves accuracy relative to a first-cumulant approximation. Building on the price decomposition, we decompose duration and convexity into component-level contributions and express the condition for negative convexity in terms of their second-order interest rate sensitivities. Finally, phase diagrams across interest rate levels and refinancing sensitivities identify the regions where negative convexity occurs and the corresponding sign boundaries. The negative-convexity region expands as the long-run mean of refinancing sensitivity increases. Negative convexity can arise even when no refinancing incentive exists initially, while its magnitude may vary nonmonotonically with the interest rate level. These findings explain negative MBS convexity through the sensitivities of its price components and quantitatively characterize the conditions governing its occurrence. △ Less

The results indicate that coupling spectral particular so… ▽ More Physics-informed holomorphic neural networks (PIHNNs) exploit complex-analytic representations to satisfy homogeneous PDEs by construction, but their extension to nonhomogeneous problems is nontrivial. We propose a hybrid physics-informed holomorphic neural network (HPIHNN) framework based on a decomposition of the solution into particular and homogeneous components. The particular component is computed by a spectral method on a simple extended domain, while the homogeneous component is approximated by a PIHNN trained only on the boundary with modified boundary data. The resulting formulation avoids meshing complex geometries and retains the dimensionality-reduction advantage of holomorphic representations. The method is applied to nonhomogeneous Poisson, Stokes, biharmonic, linear elasticity, and Helmholtz equations. Numerical experiments covering complex geometries, corner singularities, and nearly incompressible regimes demonstrate that the proposed method accurately resolves the corresponding solutions and, in the considered tests, generally achieves lower errors and shorter training times than standard PINNs. The results indicate that coupling spectral particular solutions with holomorphic neural approximations provides an efficient approach to nonhomogeneous PDEs on complex domains. △ Less

In this work, we present a computational framework for visualizing geodesics and geodesic spheres on a three-dimensional Riemannian manifold equipped with the metric $g=\text{diag}(1,x^2,1), x>0$. The associated Christoffel symbols and curvature tensors are computed symbolically, and the resulting geodesic equations are solved using both \texttt{SciPy}'s numerical integration method and a Physics-Informed Neural Network (PINN). The two solutions are compared to assess the accuracy of the neural network approximation. We further develop geodesic spike diagrams and surface visualizations to illustrate the deformation of geodesic spheres relative to their Euclidean counterparts. Intrinsic Riemannian surface area and volume are estimated numerically using the induced metric and the Riemannian volume density, respectively. For the chosen metric and a geodesic ball of radius one, the computed area and volume are approximately $0.362\%$ and $0.364\%$ smaller than the corresponding Euclidean values at the base grid resolution. A grid-refinement study is also performed to examine the sensitivity of these estimates to numerical resolution. The proposed framework demonstrates how symbolic differential geometry, numerical integration, and PINNs can be combined to investigate and visualize geometric structures associated with Riemannian metrics. △ Less

Existing methods address this challenge through dense full-joint modeling, which incurs substantial computational cost and hinders learning transferable motion patterns across diverse skeletal topologies.     Abstract: …and proofs of the existence and uniqueness of the numerical solutions. Finally, numerical examples, including some 'real' simulations, are presented to verify the theoretical findings and demon… ▽ More In this paper, we propose and analyze two novel structure-preserving projection schemes for the incompressible miscible displacement problem in porous media. These schemes are specifically designed to preserve two essential physical properties--concentration bounds and the mass balance identity, and are accompanied by a rigorous error analysis for the projection approach. Both schemes are second-order accurate in space but differ in temporal accuracy: one is first-order and the other is second-order. Methodologically, both schemes comprise a prediction step for the concentration variable, based on a second-order modified upwind discretization together with an implicit-explicit finite difference approximation, followed by a projection (or correction) step that enforces the desired physical constraints, namely bounds and mass conservation. For the first-order temporal scheme, the velocity and pressure are solved at the first step on staggered spatial grids with explicit treatment of concentration. In contrast, for the second-order scheme, to ensure well-posedness and to suppress non-physical oscillations in the simulation, the velocity and pressure are solved at the final step with the concentration treated implicitly. A rigorous numerical theoretical framework is established for the first-order scheme, encompassing optimal-order error estimates, stability, and proofs of the existence and uniqueness of the numerical solutions. The numerical analysis framework is further extended to the more involved second-order scheme, and the corresponding theoretical results are briefly outlined. Finally, numerical examples, including some 'real' simulations, are presented to verify the theoretical findings and demonstrate the strong performance of the proposed schemes. In particular, the occurrence of viscous fingering is successfully captured and confirmed by the numerical results. △ Less

However, intra-image and inter-image aggregation serve different roles: the former should propagate spatial evidence within each image while retaining structured two-dimensional dependencies, whereas the latter should gather information from plausible corresponding regions while limiting noise from unrelated locations. We propose WovenAnchor Matcher (WAM), a semi-dense matching framework based on role-specialized context aggregation. WAM introduces two complementary operators. WovenMamba performs intra-image aggregation through horizontal-then-vertical state-space propagation, allowing vertical updates to operate on horizontally contextualized features and thereby encouraging a structured two-dimensional receptive-field bias. AnchorCrossAttention performs inter-image aggregation by using cross-attention to gather context from plausible corresponding regions across the image pair. It performs this exchange over local representative anchors that aggregate feature evidence within each window. On MegaDepth, WAM achieves 66.1 pose AUC@5$^\circ$, improving on the JamMa baseline by 2.0 AUC points under the same evaluation setup. Ablations show that removing or replacing either specialized component reduces accuracy, providing evidence that intra-image propagation and inter-image retrieval benefit from role-specific aggregation designs. Code and pretrained weights are available at https://github.com/mmaiLab/WovenAnchorMatcher. △ Less

It is a data-driven approach to downscale reduced-order models to solve PDEs efficiently and accurately. Standard multi-fidelity learning focuses on one operator or PDE; given a new PDE, retraining using additional coarse-fine solution pairs may be required, which is costly. In this work, we propose an in-context multi-fidelity learning framework for learning multiple multi-fidelity operators with one shared model. The model is trained using paired low- and high-fidelity examples as demonstrations from multiple operators, together with a new coarse-scale solution query, to predict the corresponding fine-scale solution. Once trained, given a new PDE operator, a few (fewer than five) coarse-fine demonstration pairs, and a low-cost coarse query, the model predicts the corresponding fine-scale solution without fine-tuning. When the target operator appears in the training dataset but has a different prediction goal, such as predicting beyond the training time interval, no additional demonstrations are needed. Instead, the model uses training coarse-fine pairs from that PDE and information from other training operators. Testing then only requires generating a low-cost coarse-scale solution query, without fine-tuning. Notably, our smallest model contains only 255,364 trainable parameters, corresponding to approximately 456 parameters per training operator for 560 operators. △ Less

We consider continuous functions $f:\mathbb R\to[-1,1]$ that are $1$-periodic, even, strictly decreasing on $[0,1/2]$, and satisfy $f(0)=1$ and $f(t+1/2)=-f(t)$. This class includes the cosine and the triangle wave. When $ρ_α^{-1}$ is an integer, we obtain \begin{align} F_α(n) &\leq C_αn^{\log_2((1-ρ_α)/(1-2ρ_α))}ρ_α^n, \end{align} and hence the limit equals $ρ_α$. Otherwise, the limit belongs to the interval $[ρ_α,\lfloorρ_α^{-1}\rfloor^{-1}]$. The upper bound in the reciprocal case uses a cyclic covering construction previously studied in the context of circular graphs.     A Differentiable, GPU-Accelerated Finite-Element Formulation in JAX for Large-Deformation Shells with Contact: Application to Patient-Specific Aortic Valve Simulation

    Authors: Mohsen Nakhaei, Alison M. Pouch, Matthew A. Jolley, Wensi Wu

    Abstract: …and one bicuspid) achieved final relative equilibrium residuals below 10^-4. Median GPU runtimes were approximately 15-25 minutes, with 5.3-6.5-fold speedups relative to corresponding CPU runs on the tested hardware. These results provide component-level verification and demonstrate GPU-accelerated forward valve simulation in the tested cases, supporting fut… ▽ More Patient-specific aortic-valve models can help assess how tissue properties and geometry influence leaflet deformation and coaptation, with potential to inform intervention planning. Large deformations, near-incompressibility, and evolving contact make these simulations computationally demanding and can challenge nonlinear convergence. Differentiable, GPU-accelerated methods offer efficient derivative evaluation and parallel computation, but their matrix-free application to soft-tissue shells with contact remains less established. We developed a forward solver using tatva's energy-based finite-element framework in JAX. The solver combines finite-strain extensible-director shells, hyperelastic constitutive models, and a finite-range repulsive contact energy. Nonlinear equilibrium is solved using a regularized, matrix-free Newton-Krylov method with two-level preconditioning. Automatic differentiation evaluates tangent-vector products without forming or storing the primary tangent matrix, while GPU execution parallelizes element and quadrature-point computations. Constitutive, shell, contact, and self-contact responses were assessed against analytical, semi-analytical, and published benchmarks. At a contact-free plate equilibrium, implicit sensitivities of the converged apex displacement to constitutive parameters, thickness, and pressure agreed with finite differences from independent nonlinear simulations. Simulations of three patient-specific aortic-valve models (two tricuspid and one bicuspid) achieved final relative equilibrium residuals below 10^-4. Median GPU runtimes were approximately 15-25 minutes, with 5.3-6.5-fold speedups relative to corresponding CPU runs on the tested hardware. These results provide component-level verification and demonstrate GPU-accelerated forward valve simulation in the tested cases, supporting future gradient-based inverse studies. △ Less

The scheme is based on a continuous pseudostress-velocity augmented dual-mixed finite element formulation, in which the Dirichlet datum is imposed naturally, whereas the prescribed normal trace of the pseudostress is incorporated into the $H(div,)$ stress space. We establish the well-posedness of the continuous formulation by means of a classical lifting argument and its corresponding homogeneous problem. Moreover, in the case of homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions and general Neumann data, we establish the stability of the Nitsche scheme. Under mild regularity assumptions on the exact solution, we prove optimal convergence rates in an energy norm. Finally, numerical experiments in two and three dimensions confirm the theoretical convergence rates and assess the performance of the proposed scheme for non-solenoidal convective fields and solutions exhibiting boundary layers. △ Less

    Sample Complexity for Generative Learning of Quantum Measurement Data

    Authors: Shuhan Zhang, Yi-Zhuang You

    Abstract: …. Bulk-site results are consistent with the corresponding finite-sample predictions. These results connect the structure of quantum measurement distributions to the model capacity justified by finite data. ▽ More Generative learning of a quantum measurement distribution from finite data requires choosing a model capacity that balances representation accuracy against estimation cost. We develop a bias-variance framework that decomposes the expected population Kullback-Leibler error at model capacity $s$ and sample size $N$ as $\mathcal E(s,N)=B(s)+V(s,N)$, with representation bias $B$ and a variance term $V$ capturing the mean estimation error. In the well-sampled regime, $V(s,N)\simeq[NC(s)]^{-1}$, and exponential or algebraic laws for $B$ and $C$ yield four scaling templates for the optimal error, from inverse powers of $N$ to inverse powers of $\ln N$. For local autoregressive models, the context radius sets the model capacity, and $B$ is the summed conditional mutual information lost by truncating the context. For a smoothed histogram learner, we derive an exact finite-sample estimation cost that accounts for rarely observed contexts and recovers the parameter-count law in the well-sampled regime. For transverse-field Ising Born distributions, the independently evaluated bias and estimation cost predict both the optimal context radius and the optimal error. In the well-sampled regime, the estimation cost grows exponentially with context radius. Its competition with approximately exponential bias decay in the gapped regimes predicts power-law error decay in $N$, while algebraic bias decay at criticality predicts asymptotic inverse-power decay in $\ln N$. Bulk-site results are consistent with the corresponding finite-sample predictions. These results connect the structure of quantum measurement distributions to the model capacity justified by finite data. △ Less

    An Improved Johnson Bound for List-Decoding with Hints

    Authors: Swastik Kopparty, Harry Sha

    Abstract: This paper studies when list-decodability guarantees for error-correcting codes of good distance can go beyond the classical Johnson radius and gives a corresponding efficient list-decoding algorithm for Reed-Solomon codes up to this new radius. The Johnson bound shows that any error-correcting code with distance $δ$ is automatically list-decodable with smal… ▽ More This paper studies when list-decodability guarantees for error-correcting codes of good distance can go beyond the classical Johnson radius and gives a corresponding efficient list-decoding algorithm for Reed-Solomon codes up to this new radius. The Johnson bound shows that any error-correcting code with distance $δ$ is automatically list-decodable with small list size up to a certain radius $J(δ)$ around any received word $r$. We show that if the received word $r$ is known to have some codewords that are somewhat close to it (we call these ``hints"), then $C$ is list-decodable with small list size up to an even larger radius around $r$. We determine the optimal list-decoding radius in this setting for single and multiple hints over both large and small alphabets and show that this radius is the optimum of a natural convex program. Remarkably, even a single hint that is $(J(δ)-ε)$-close to $r$ improves the list-decoding radius around $r$ to $J(δ)+ε'$. Finally, we give an efficient algorithm, based on the weighted Guruswami-Sudan decoder, that list-decodes Reed-Solomon codes up to this optimal radius. △ Less

    Ornstein--Uhlenbeck spirals: Absorption Probabilities and Conic Intrinsic Volumes

    Authors: Zakhar Kabluchko

    Abstract: …volumes of the closed positive hulls of deterministic Ornstein--Uhlenbeck spirals and their circular analogues in Hilbert spaces. After suitable rescaling, the small-time limits of these conic intrinsic volumes recover the formulas of Gao--Vitale and Gao for… ▽ More Let $(U_t)_{t\in\mathbb R}$ be a stationary Ornstein--Uhlenbeck process in $\mathbb R^d$ whose coordinate processes $(U_t^{(i)})_{t\in\mathbb R}$, $1\le i\le d$, are independent, centered, and have covariance function $\mathbb E [U_s^{(i)}U_t^{(i)}]=e^{-|t-s|/2}$. For every $T>0$, we obtain an explicit formula for the probability that the convex hull $\mathrm{conv}\{U_t:0\le t\le T\}$ contains the origin. We obtain a similar formula for a circular Ornstein--Uhlenbeck loop whose covariance is the normalized $T$-periodization of this kernel. For each fixed dimension $d$, we determine the precise long-time asymptotics of the non-absorption probabilities, which decay as explicit constants times $T^{d-1} e^{-T/2}$ as $T\to\infty$. We also show that, as $d\to\infty$, the absorption probabilities in both models undergo a transition from $0$ to $1$ around $T=2d$, with a common Gaussian limiting profile on the scale $\sqrt d$. We also obtain explicit formulas for the probability that $σZ$ belongs to the convex hull of Brownian motion or Brownian bridge on $[0,1]$ in $\mathbb R^d$, where $Z$ is an independent standard Gaussian vector and $σ\ge0$. The large-$σ$ asymptotics recover the known formulas for the expected volumes of these convex hulls. Finally, we compute the conic intrinsic volumes of the closed positive hulls of deterministic Ornstein--Uhlenbeck spirals and their circular analogues in Hilbert spaces. Their Grassmann angles coincide with the corresponding Ornstein--Uhlenbeck absorption probabilities. After suitable rescaling, the small-time limits of these conic intrinsic volumes recover the formulas of Gao--Vitale and Gao for the intrinsic volumes of the Brownian body and its Brownian-bridge analogue. △ Less

We introduce the notions of Weyl power spectrum and Weyl-conjugation autocorrelation for the Weyl-operator quantities nderlying the Shor--Laflamme weight enumerators. Representing these quantities as formal vectors, we show that they form a symplectic Fourier transform pair, from which the quantum MacWilliams identity follows by applying the Hamming-weight functional. This framework extends to a family of twisted Fourier transforms, including the Rains shadow transform. We show that identical-copy Bell sampling corresponds to a twisted autocorrelation related to the shadow enumerator through Weyl translation and Hamming projection. We further prove that all twisted Fourier transforms are Weyl translations of either the ordinary MacWilliams transform or the Rains shadow transform. This two-class reduction implies that the twists yield no additional independent coefficientwise positivity constraints under compatible projections. Nevertheless, the framework leads to refined MacWilliams and shadow identities that retain more Fourier information than ordinary Hamming weight enumerators. We employ these refined identities in linear programming, together with simplex mapping and shadow-coset counting, to exclude surviving candidate weight distributions. These techniques yield tighter bounds for binary and qutrit quantum codes, including nonexistence results for specific code parameters. △ Less

Among eight methods, Bagged PCMCI+ had the highest mean AUPRC on sparse VAR, an autocorrelated structural process, and DREAM3; CUTS+ led on Lorenz-96. Wind-tunnel and river-discharge applications illustrate differences between inferred graphs without establishing causal accuracy. We prove the computational identities and a restricted population adjacency result for fastC-GC$^*$. Causal interpretation requires the stated graphical assumptions; validity of the implemented tests additionally requires a suitable shift-invariant null law. Empirical null rejection rates informed the conditional threshold $β= 0.005$, without establishing general test validity or graph-wide error control. △ Less

    Authors: Shenglong Yao, Zirui Liu, Xiangchi Yuan, Wenke Lee

    Abstract: …how intrinsic reasoning, active information acquisition, and external knowledge each contribute to interprocedural binary analysis. We evaluate five recent LLMs and their corresponding agents. Our results reveal a wide separation across current models and agents, with program-level verifiable success rates ranging from 23% to 81%. They also show that greater… ▽ More Recent benchmarks show that LLMs achieve strong performance in both function-level binary understanding and end-to-end binary reverse engineering tasks, but whether LLMs can reason over interprocedural semantics in compiled binaries remains unclear. Function-level benchmarks offer limited visibility into cross-function relationships, while whole-program benchmarks typically evaluate final outcomes of tasks that may be solvable from local clues alone, making it difficult to disentangle interprocedural reasoning from exploration, tool use, execution, and retrieval. To address this limitation, we introduce ICALL-BENCH, a benchmark that directly evaluates whether LLMs and agents can understand and reason over interprocedural semantics in binary programs, using indirect-call target recovery as a measurable probe. ICALL-BENCH covers 50 complete real-world C/C++ programs spanning different complexity levels and multiple optimization configurations, with an average of 74,746 lines of code and 941 functions per program. We further introduce three analysis regimes spanning model-only reasoning, static tool-augmented agents, and Internet-enabled open-world agents to examine how intrinsic reasoning, active information acquisition, and external knowledge each contribute to interprocedural binary analysis. We evaluate five recent LLMs and their corresponding agents. Our results reveal a wide separation across current models and agents, with program-level verifiable success rates ranging from 23% to 81%. They also show that greater information access does not consistently lead to better results, with particularly limited gains on the hardest cases, where deeper interprocedural reasoning remains the primary bottleneck. Uneven performance across models and frequent verifiable failures even under an optimistic evaluation indicate that robust binary interprocedural reasoning remains an open challenge. △ Less

We ask what happens to food similarity beyond identity: how different visual representations organize foods into relational neighborhoods, what culinary relationships these neighborhoods reveal or obscure, and how consistently these structures emerge across representations. Indian cuisine provides a challenging setting in which visually similar foods may have different culinary identities, while foods within a culinary family can vary substantially in appearance. At the dish level, aggregation reveals common visual structure despite local variation, but this structure corresponds less strongly with recipe-derived similarity. These findings show that recognition performance alone cannot characterize relational structure: food similarity depends on representation, modality, and abstraction level rather than being a fixed property of the food collection.     The Geometry of Quantum Teleportation in Phase Space

    Authors: Jasel Berra-Montiel, Alberto Molgado, Mar Sánchez-Córdova

    Abstract: We formulate the standard quantum teleportation protocol within the framework of deformation quantization, employing the Stratonovich--Weyl (SW) correspondence and the associated star product on the coadjoint orbits of $SU(2)$. Each qubit is represented by a spherical phase space $S^2$ endowed with the Kirillov--Kostant--Souriau (KKS) symplectic structure. W… ▽ More We formulate the standard quantum teleportation protocol within the framework of deformation quantization, employing the Stratonovich--Weyl (SW) correspondence and the associated star product on the coadjoint orbits of $SU(2)$. Each qubit is represented by a spherical phase space $S^2$ endowed with the Kirillov--Kostant--Souriau (KKS) symplectic structure. Within this setting, we explicitly construct the phase space representations of the entangled Bell resource, the controlled-NOT and Hadamard operators, the projective measurements, and the conditional Pauli corrections. We demonstrate how the quantum information initially encoded in the Wigner function for the input qubit is redistributed through bipartite and tripartite phase space correlations. Upon consideration of a conditional unitary transformation, the measurement outcome produces a Wigner function on the receiver's coadjoint orbit that is related to the input by an outcome-dependent rotation, which can subsequently be inverted through the implementation of an appropriate Pauli correction. The covariance of the SW correspondence provides a geometric interpretation of this process, in which quantum information is redistributed and reconstructed without transferring the underlying symplectic geometry. Our formulation can also be extended to study quantum teleportation in more general phase space geometries equipped with suitable star products. △ Less

Their emergence has been extensively investigated with a particular emphasis on the role of network topology, both in conventional pairwise interactions and, more recently, in higher-order interaction networks. Here, we propose a generalized framework for neuronal hypergraphs incorporating higher-order interactions through attractive chemical and repulsive electrical coupling. While higher-order interactions often support broader chimera regions, we show that clique projections can also preserve or promote chimera states, even in regimes where the corresponding hypergraphs exhibit fully incoherent dynamics. Therefore, these findings suggest that chimera formation is not only influenced by the topology or by the interaction order but also by the form of the coupling functions and the balance between attractive and repulsive interactions, offering further insights into collective dynamics in neuronal networks. △ Less

    Authors: Diksha Gupta, Rieke Fruengel, Stefan Heimersheim

    Abstract: …some concepts as multi-dimensional manifolds instead of single directions. Despite its striking circular… ▽ More Recent work has hypothesised that large language models may represent some concepts as multi-dimensional manifolds instead of single directions. Using these manifolds to interpret or steer representations often assumes that moving along them corresponds to a continuous change in the represented concept. In this work, we test this continuous manifold assumption on the prototypical weekday manifold. Despite its striking circular geometry, we find that the weekday representations behave more like seven discrete states arranged in a circle than a continuous ring. First, we show that local sensitivity has seven minima aligned with the weekdays, producing a step-like mapping between representation and behaviour under on-manifold steering. Second, we test whether any concepts are represented along the manifold outside the seven discrete states, and find neither time of day, uncertainty, nor natural text populate the intermediate space. Together, these results challenge the continuous interpretation of the weekday manifold and motivate testing whether movement along other representation manifolds correspond to meaningful changes in model representations and behaviour. △ Less

    Abstract: …fermionic representation in which this topological bulk sector is described instead by conventional symmetry breaking. Focusing away from special exactly solvable lattice limits, we develop a continuum field-theoretic description of the generic finite-density interacting regime, where doping nonperturbatively modifies the competition between superconducting pairing and commensurate charge ordering. We show that the phase diagram consists of an intermediate incommensurate Luttinger liquid between the topological superconducting and commensurate charge-density-wave Mott phases, as well as a trivial band-insulating regime at large chemical potential. We also construct a dual continuum fermionic representation in which this topological bulk sector is described instead by conventional symmetry breaking. This gives a continuum version of the SPT--SSB correspondence between different fermionic representations. Overall, our results provide an analytical description of one-dimensional interacting topological superconductors, from the competition between topology and commensurability and the resulting quantum criticality to the dual symmetry-breaking description of the topological phase. △ Less

We present MoireKP, an automated workflow for constructing compact continuum models from localized-orbital ab initio Hamiltonians.     Authors: Innes Bigaran, André de Gouvêa, Tao Han, Ian Low, Yang Ma, Zahra Tabrizi, Keping Xie

    Abstract: …vector bosons when their masses are kinematically accessible, while their off-shell effects are captured by the corresponding effective operators. The radiation of soft $W^\pm$ gauge bosons from the muon beams gives rise to collisions of $μ^+ν$, $μ^-\barν$ and $ν\barν$ at center-of-mass energies close to that of the $μ^+μ^-$ collisions in a controlled and theoretically predictable setting. We focus on the leading partonic processes $μ^+μ^- \to \ell^+\ell'^-$, $ν_μμ^+\to ν_\ell \ell'^+$, $\barν_μμ^- \to \barν_{\ell} \ell'^-$ and $ν_μ\bar ν_μ\to \ell^+\ell'^-$ ($\ell, \ell'=e, μ, τ$). These are related to one another at high energies by the electroweak $SU(2)$ gauge symmetry, and therefore probe different components of the same underlying electroweak dynamics. These processes open qualitatively new windows to neutrino interactions at unprecedented energies, and open new windows on physics beyond the Standard Model. We demonstrate the combined sensitivity and the complementarity of the different purely-leptonic collisions using both effective field theory and simplified models of $W'^\pm$ and $Z'$ vector bosons. The radiative return mechanism leads to resonant production of the $W^{\prime\pm}$ and $Z'$ vector bosons when their masses are kinematically accessible, while their off-shell effects are captured by the corresponding effective operators. In particular, we show that supplementing the dominant charged-lepton collisions with neutrino-initiated collisions significantly improves the ability to resolve degeneracies among different effective operators, even for operators to which the neutrino-initiated processes are not directly sensitive. Including these channels is therefore essential for properly characterizing the structure of new physics. △ Less

    Authors: Siyoon Jin, Dahyun Chung, Honggyu An, Sangbeom Lim, Wonjin Nam, Jaewoo Jung, WonJun Moon, Seungryong Kim

    Abstract: Cross-view object correspondence identifies the same object across views. However, existing methods often rely on frame-wise matching or sequential tracking, which can propagate correspondence errors under appearance changes or occlusions. Therefore, we introduce cross-view temporal object correspondence, which requires preserving object identity across both views and time from a query mask. To address this, we propose XTrack, a unified framework which repurposes a pretrained video DiT with semantic and spatiotemporal priors for RGB-to-mask latent transport, jointly modeling cross-view matching and temporal tracking. Thus, we introduce focal cross-view attention alignment, hard negative conditioning, and local context crop strategies to ensure reliable grounding. Finally, beyond these architectural enhancements, we also tackle the lack of dense annotation by introducing a pseudo-label curation pipeline that converts sparse Ego-Exo4D annotations into dense supervision to enable effective video-level training. Our results demonstrate the effectiveness of XTrack as a unified framework, ensuring robust temporal consistency and identity preservation. △ Less

Similarly to the deterministic case, lower-$C^2$ normal integrands are defined as the pointwise maxima over a compact random index set of functions that are twice-continuously differentiable in the decision variable with an integrable bounded Hessian. Our main result is a local value function characterization of this class of functions, namely, that they satisfy a local sufficient ascent property almost surely. Equivalently, they differ from a convex normal integrand by a random quadratic. The upper-$C^2$ theory follows by a change of sign, and delivers the corresponding lower envelopes, the form in which these integrands appear as second-stage value functions of stochastic programs with compact recourse. We also show that for this class integrability of the function values, of the local Lipschitz moduli, and of the minimum-norm Clarke subgradients are equivalent. We then study smooth integrands, finite lower envelopes, difference-of-convex integrands, Moreau and forward--backward envelopes, compositions with a smooth map, monotone convex compositions, and compact-recourse stochastic programs within these classes of nonconvex stochastic functions.     Linear-quadratic mean field games with Volterra dynamics

    Authors: Niccolò Fontana, Mattia Martini, Huyên Pham

    Abstract: …horizons and, when the control does not act on the diffusion coefficient, on arbitrary horizons under a monotonicity condition on the interaction. As a consequence, the feedback control of any mean field equilibrium, when implemented by each ag… ▽ More We study a class of linear-quadratic mean field games in which the state of each agent follows a stochastic Volterra equation of convolution type. The resulting dynamics are non-Markovian and account for memory effects through a completely monotone kernel, while the mean field interaction enters both the drift and diffusion coefficients. Exploiting a Markovian lifting of the Volterra dynamics, which restores the Markov property at the price of moving to an infinite-dimensional state space, we characterize the optimal response of a representative agent for a given population mean through a system of Riccati equations in Banach spaces, thereby obtaining a feedback representation of the optimal control. We then study the consistency condition defining the mean field equilibrium, which reduces to a forward-backward fixed point equation for the equilibrium mean: we prove existence and uniqueness of the equilibrium on short horizons and, when the control does not act on the diffusion coefficient, on arbitrary horizons under a monotonicity condition on the interaction. Finally, we consider the corresponding finite-player game and establish a quantitative propagation of chaos result. As a consequence, the feedback control of any mean field equilibrium, when implemented by each agent on its own state, yields an $\varepsilon_N$-Nash equilibrium for the $N$-player game, with an explicit rate as the number of agents tends to infinity. △ Less

By exploiting the knot-clamping boundary property, we propose a theoretically grounded mechanism for exact control-point prediction directly informed by inertial kinematics along the trajectory, with knot placement adapted to motion and measurement availability. Within an iterated extended Kalman filter, we perform this prediction using a subset of inertial measurements. A subsequent reset step preserves trajectory consistency as the knots extend. We propose an analytic event simulator that exploits what event cameras fundamentally measure - moving contrast edges - to synthesize event streams directly from scene geometry and ego-motion without visual rendering. Our simulator generates a 64x64 event observation in 17 microseconds on a single GPU - over 400x faster than a state-of-the-art rendering pipeline - enabling end-to-end reinforcement learning for goal-directed obstacle avoidance to converge in under 30 minutes - three orders of magnitude faster than training on rendered events. Policies transfer zero-shot to physically based event simulations (ray-traced Isaac Sim). Our policy reaches 83% success in cluttered courses unseen during training (mid-difficulty, traversability probability approx. 0.57), at 2.4 m/s average goal velocity, and retains 70% in a simulated texture-dense indoor hall. Furthermore, we quantized the policy to a 1.2 MB int8 network running fully onboard a 56-gram Crazyflie equipped with a Prophesee GenX320 event camera and an OpenMV N6 microcontroller board. Without any fine-tuning, external processing, or positioning, the policy achieves an average success rate of 82% (5/5, 5/6, 4/6 flights) across three real-world obstacle courses of increasing difficulty. This metadata is rarely a clean disease signal, since a single profile also contains symptoms, age, sex, missing responses, and recruitment information.     Authors: Weijun Fang, Jing Qiu, Shu-Tao Xia, Fang-Wei Fu

    Abstract: …, resolving the corresponding exact linear-repair case of Open Problem 20 posed by Ramkumar et al. in their survey on codes for distributed storage. We further give a finite formula for $G_s(n)$ and determine the exact threshold for its closed-form expression in terms of the first $n$ primes. For fixed $k$ and $d$, we prove… ▽ More In this paper, we study the minimum subpacketization of $(n,k)$ Reed-Solomon (RS) codes over $\mathbb{F}_{q^\ell}$ admitting cut-set-optimal exact $\mathbb{F}_q$-linear repair of every coordinate from any $d$ helpers. For $3\le k<d\le n-1$, write $s=d+1-k$ and denote this minimum by $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)$. Using divisibility and relative-degree constraints on cross-ratio fields of evaluation sets, we establish $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)\ge sG_s(d+1)$, where $G_s(m)$ is the minimum product of $m$ pairwise coprime integers, each at least $s$. Let $Δ_s(n,d)$ be the minimum of $lcm(a_1,\ldots,a_n)$ over positive integers $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ satisfying $lcm(a_j:j\in J\cup\{i\})\ge s~lcm(a_j:j\in J)$ for every $d$-subset $J\subseteq[n]$ and every $i\in[n]\setminus J$. We construct RS codes with subpacketization $sΔ_s(n,d)$ and further prove that $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)=sΔ_s(n,d)=sG_s(n)$ whenever $d(d+2)\geq 4(n+1)$, for every prime power $q$. In particular, this determines the minimum subpacketization in the full-helper case $d=n-1$, resolving the corresponding exact linear-repair case of Open Problem 20 posed by Ramkumar et al. in their survey on codes for distributed storage. We further give a finite formula for $G_s(n)$ and determine the exact threshold for its closed-form expression in terms of the first $n$ primes. For fixed $k$ and $d$, we prove $\log\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)= Θ_{k,d}(\log n\log\log n)$ as $n\to\infty$, using divisor-function estimates and cover-free families. An affine-plane construction shows that $Δ_s(n,d)<G_s(n)$ for some parameters, so $sG_s(n)$ is not a universal formula. We conjecture that $\ell_{min}(n,k,d;q)=sΔ_s(n,d)$ for every prime power $q$ and all $3\le k<d\le n-1$. △ Less

Language models make that proposal measurable by converting each item into an embedding, a list of numbers, similar for similar meanings. We introduce semanticfa, an R package for semantic factor analysis, which factors item-embedding similarities. It also interprets factors, flags redundant items, builds short forms, and screens new items. A worked example analyzes the 50-item International Personality Item Pool Big-Five Factor Markers, embedded with Qwen3-Embedding-8B, and compares the results with a factor analysis of 874,434 respondents. Each factor's congruence with its best-matching human factor (1 = identical) ranged from .92 for Openness to .52 for Agreeableness. Item pairs more similar in meaning tended to be answered more alike (r = .46 with raw responses), but same-domain reverse-keyed opposites, answered oppositely, were about as similar as cross-domain pairs (.58 vs. .57). Sign-flipping reverse-keyed items' embeddings did not improve agreement with raw responses and worsened it with reverse-scored ones. With five factors, a 25-item short form matched the domains better than all 200 random five-per-domain sets, but parallel analysis kept only three. We propose semantic-behavioral coherence, the agreement between semantic and response structures, as a measurable property of a scale, given an embedding model. Semantic factor analysis complements, and does not replace, response-based factor analysis. △ Less

    Abstract: …spin polarization. Its leading angular pattern counter-rotates by half the in-plane Néel-vector rotation angle. Analytic… ▽ More Conventional altermagnetic spin splitting arises from staggered exchange and sublattice-dependent anisotropic hopping without requiring spin-orbit coupling (SOC). Here we derive a low-energy effective model showing that SOC and staggered exchange can jointly activate additional spin splitting on momentum planes where the nonrelativistic contribution vanishes. In hexagonal $α$-MnTe, this SOC-activated contribution produces d-wave spin splitting near $Γ$ with out-of-plane spin polarization. Its leading angular pattern counter-rotates by half the in-plane Néel-vector rotation angle. First-principles spin-transport calculations reveal a corresponding approximate half-angle locking between the Néel-vector orientation and the electric-field directions maximizing the signed longitudinal and transverse spin conductivities. Analytical transport calculations based on the effective model trace this locking to the same angular coupling between momentum and the Néel vector that governs the spin-splitting pattern. Our findings connect the microscopic mechanism of SOC-activated altermagnetic spin splitting to directional spin transport, providing a theoretical basis for probing Néel-vector orientation and controlling spin-current generation. △ Less

    Authors: Iulian Lucau, Adelin-George Voicu, Simon Munk Christensen

    Abstract: …trust. Drawing on trust, technology-acceptance and digital-divide theory, it is a descriptive, contrasting-case analysis of harmonised data: GenAI us… ▽ More Generative AI (GenAI) has spread unevenly across Europe, and it is unclear whether higher use brings higher trust. This paper compares Denmark and Romania, the EU's highest and lowest GenAI users, and asks whether the difference in public and institutional trust between them corresponds to the difference in trust in AI. Drawing on trust, technology-acceptance and digital-divide theory, it is a descriptive, contrasting-case analysis of harmonised data: GenAI usage comes from Eurostat, trust in AI more generally from the KPMG/Melbourne global study and public and institutional trust from the Standard Eurobarometer. In 2025, 48.4% of Danes aged 16-74 had used GenAI in the past three months, against 17.8% of Romanians. Use falls with age and rises with education in both countries, and the relative gaps are far larger in Romania; the patterns remain visible within the published age-by-education categories. Trust in AI does not follow usage: Romanians trust AI as much as Danes or slightly more, although Denmark leads by 28 to 45 percentage points on trust in government, parliament and the legal system. The Denmark-Romania comparison thus does not support a simple country-level correspondence between general institutional trust and trust in AI; in KPMG's pooled model, trust in AI is tied most closely to confidence in AI governance. The observed usage difference is more consistent with disparities in general digital skills and opportunities for work- and education-related use than with differences in trust. However, the available data cannot identify the independent effects of these factors. The results rest on two cases and non-identical constructs, and call for comparable microdata and disclosure experiments. △ Less

We first analyze the geometric properties of these solutions, including their regular central structure and horizon configurations, and show how the interplay between the T-duality scale $\ell_0$ and the electric charge $Q$ determines the transition between black hole and horizonless geometries. We then study the motion of massive and massless particles through the corresponding effective potentials, examining the existence and stability of circular orbits and the behavior of their characteristic radii. We further investigate the tidal forces experienced by observers in radial free fall, focusing on the behavior of the radial and angular components, their extrema and sign changes, and the location of these characteristic points relative to the horizon structure of the geometry. We show that the T-duality regularization renders the tidal forces finite in the central region and gives rise to different stretching and compression regimes, including a region of simultaneous compression in both the radial and angular directions, while the electric charge modifies the strength and location of these tidal features. Finally, we analyze the evolution of the geodesic deviation vector for two sets of initial conditions. We find that both the T-duality scale and the electric charge tend to suppress the accumulated radial stretching, whereas the angular deformation is considerably less sensitive to the charge. These results show how the quantum regularization induced by T-duality and the presence of electric charge modify the orbital dynamics and tidal response of these geometries in the strong-field regime.
